**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Adrian Andrews states that she was the one who knocked the flower vase over, causing it to fall onto the guitar case (Testimony 5). However, Evidence 7 describes the Guitar Case as being found next to the victim with some water only on top of the lid. This suggests that the water from the vase would likely have splashed onto more than just the top of the lid if the vase had fallen onto the case.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

None of the spatial propositions directly address the scenario of a vase falling onto a guitar case or the distribution of water on the guitar case.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Since no applicable propositions exist, we skip this step.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial description of the water on the guitar case. If Andrews knocked over the vase onto the guitar case, it is reasonable to expect that the water would have splashed onto multiple parts of the case, not just the top of the lid. This contradicts the evidence that states the water is only on top of the lid, suggesting that the vase did not fall directly onto the guitar case.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Looking at the spatial propositions, Prop 3 seems to be relevant to the Magenta Flower and the Red Raincoat.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 3 states: "If the Magenta Flower is torn from the victim's clothes and the victim's clothes are covered by the Red Raincoat then the Magenta Flower is inside the Red Raincoat."

According to Evidence 6, the Magenta Flower was torn from the victim's clothes. According to Evidence 22, the Red Raincoat was worn by the victim, and her fingerprints and blood were found on the hood. This implies that the victim's clothes were covered by the Red Raincoat.

However, Evidence 16 describes the ringleader wearing a magenta flower, indicating that the Magenta Flower was not inside the Red Raincoat but rather on the ringleader.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 3 does apply and reveals the contradiction. The spatial reasoning is that if the Magenta Flower was torn from the victim's clothes and those clothes were covered by the Red Raincoat, then logically, the Magenta Flower should have been inside the Red Raincoat. However, the ringleader is described as wearing the Magenta Flower, which contradicts the implication of Proposition 3.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial arrangement of the characters and the nature of the attack. According to the photo description in Testimony 6, Valerie was facing left, which means she was facing towards the west side of the bridge. However, the autopsy report in Evidence 1 indicates she was stabbed in the back, suggesting she was facing the attacker. Since the broken end of the bridge is towards the east, Valerie could not have fallen off the broken end while facing left (west). However, Evidence 9, the Autopsy Report, states that Mr Windibank was shot from behind, indicating he fell forward. This implies that the shooter was positioned behind him, possibly outside the storeroom or in a position where they could shoot him from behind.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can use Propositions 1 and 2 to understand the spatial relationship between the storeroom and the main shop, but the key contradiction lies in Proposition 3, which states that if Windibank is shot from behind, he would fall forward.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 3: If Windibank is shot from behind then Windibank falls forward.

This proposition supports the idea that Windibank was shot from behind, which aligns with the Autopsy Report. However, if the Skulkin brothers were inside the storeroom and Windibank was also inside the storeroom, it would be difficult for someone to shoot him from behind unless the storeroom was positioned in such a way that allowed for a clear shot from behind.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Given the testimonies of the Skulkin brothers, they were inside the storeroom when the gunshot occurred. If Windibank was also inside the storeroom, it would be unlikely for him to be shot from behind unless the storeroom had a specific layout that allowed for such a scenario. Since the floor plan (Evidence 11) is not detailed enough to confirm this, the contradiction arises from the spatial arrangement implied by the testimonies and the autopsy findings.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Lance Amano states that he was held in the hideout and escaped using the underground passageway. From the evidences, we know that the Broken Prop Sword was used to jam the handle to the hideout's door from the inside (Evidence 12). This suggests that the hideout door was accessible from the inside, implying that Lance Amano was indeed inside the hideout.

However, there is no direct spatial contradiction evident from the testimonies and evidences provided. Instead, we need to look at the spatial relationships described in the spatial propositions to find a potential contradiction.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can see that Proposition 1 and Proposition 5 involve the hideout and its relationship with other locations or objects.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If Lance Amano is inside the hideout and the hideout is in front of the underground passageway and the Security Lady is inside the underground passageway then Lance Amano is in front of the Security Lady."

Proposition 5 states: "If the Broken Prop Sword is inside the hideout and the hideout is behind the Isolation Room and Oliver Deacon is inside the Isolation Room then the Broken Prop Sword is behind Oliver Deacon."

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Since Proposition 1 does not directly involve the Broken Prop Sword, we focus on Proposition 5. According to Lance's testimony, he was inside the hideout and used the underground passageway to escape. This implies that the hideout and the underground passageway are connected, and the Security Lady being inside the underground passageway would mean Lance was in front of her, which aligns with Proposition 1.

However, Proposition 5 introduces a contradiction because it states that if the Broken Prop Sword is inside the hideout and the hideout is behind the Isolation Room, then the sword is behind Oliver Deacon. Given that Oliver Deacon was found dead in the Isolation Room (Evidence 6), and the Broken Prop Sword was used to jam the hideout's door from the inside (Evidence 12), this suggests that the hideout cannot be behind the Isolation Room if the sword was inside the hideout and used to jam the door.
